=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given the first N terms A _ 1,A _ 2,\dotsc,A _ N of an infinite sequence A=(A _ 1,A _ 2,A _ 3,\dotsc) that has period N.
Determine if there exists a non-empty contiguous subsequence of this infinite sequence whose sum is S.
Here, an infinite sequence A has period N when A _ i=A _ {i-N} for every integer i>N.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N S
A _ 1 A _ 2 \dotsc A _ N

Output

If there exists a contiguous subsequence (A _ l,A _ {l+1},\dotsc,A _ r) of A for which A _ l+A _ {l+1}+\dotsb+A _ r=S, print Yes. Otherwise, print No.

Constraints


- 1\leq N\leq2\times10 ^ 5
- 1\leq A _ i\leq 10 ^ 9
- 1\leq S\leq 10 ^ {18}
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 42
3 8 4

Sample Output 1

Yes

The sequence A is (3,8,4,3,8,4,3,8,4,\dotsc).
For the subsequence (A _ 2,A _ 3,A _ 4,A _ 5,A _ 6,A _ 7,A _ 8,A _ 9)=(8,4,3,8,4,3,8,4), we have 8+4+3+8+4+3+8+4=42, so print Yes.

Sample Input 2

3 1
3 8 4

Sample Output 2

No

All elements of A are at least 3, so the sum of any non-empty contiguous subsequence is at least 3.
Thus, there is no subsequence with sum 1, so print No.

Sample Input 3

20 83298426
748 169 586 329 972 529 432 519 408 587 138 249 656 114 632 299 984 755 404 772

Sample Output 3

Yes

Sample Input 4

20 85415869
748 169 586 329 972 529 432 519 408 587 138 249 656 114 632 299 984 755 404 772

Sample Output 4

No

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, implement circular subarray sum R with two pointers. Need ensure O(N) not O(N^2). Since all positive, two-pointer works.

Let's design code:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, S = map(int, input().split())
    A = list(map(int, input().split()))
    T = sum(A)
    R = S % T
    if R == 0:
        print("Yes")
        return
    # check if R is sum of a circular contiguous subarray
    B = A + A
    l = 0
    cur = 0
    # We need find window sum R. Since positive, expand r.
    # But if R may be large, cur can exceed. We'll shrink.
    for r, val in enumerate(B):
        cur += val
        while cur > R and l <= r:
            cur -= B[l]
            l += 1
        if cur == R:
            print("Yes")
            return
    print("No")

Code:

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]; S = data[1]
    A = data[2:2+N]
    T = sum(A)
    R = S % T
    if R == 0:
        print("Yes")
        return
    B = A + A
    left = 0
    cur = 0
    for right, val in enumerate(B):
        cur += val
        while cur > R and left <= right:
            cur -= B[left]
            left += 1
        if cur == R:
            print("Yes")
            return
    print("No")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

S = QT + R, 0 <= R < T.
Then (q-Q)T = R - C.
Since R-C is in (-T+1, T-1) if C>0; if C=0, R-C in [0,T-1]. The only multiple of T in that range is 0. Thus q=Q and R=C. If C=0 and R=0, q=Q. If C>0, R=C. So yes. This is even simpler. Thus S mod T must equal C. Therefore if S not divisible, C=R>0, so b>0. Good.

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    S = data[1]
    A = data[2:2+N]
    T = sum(A)
    R = S % T
    if R == 0:
        print("Yes")
        return
    B = A + A
    left = 0
    cur = 0
    for right, val in enumerate(B):
        cur += val
        while cur > R and left <= right:
            cur -= B[left]
            left += 1
        if cur == R:
            print("Yes")
            return
    print("No")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Standard two-pointer with positive numbers: At right=N-1, if cur > R, we shrink from left until cur <= R. If the window [N-1,N] sum R, then at right=N-1, the prefix window [N-1,N-1] sum = A_N. It might be less than R, so left would not advance past N-1. Then at right=N, cur includes A_N + A_1 = R, found. If A_N alone > R, then [N-1,N] sum >R too (positive), so cannot be R. If A_N <= R, left won't pass N-1 before adding A1. More generally, if a future window sum R, the prefix before adding the new element has sum <= R (since positive), so left won't shrink past its start. Thus standard proof holds. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with the code if the input has A_i all 2, S=4N+2N+4568? R=4568 yes if N
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    S = data[1]
    A = data[2:2 + N]

    total = sum(A)
    R = S % total

    if R == 0:
        print("Yes")
        return

    B = A + A
    left = 0
    cur = 0

    for right, val in enumerate(B):
        cur += val
        while cur > R and left <= right:
            cur -= B[left]
            left += 1

        if cur == R:
            print("Yes")
            return

    print("No")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
